# HyperMesh reference pages — HyperMesh Template Function (Help 2022.3)

139 pages from the official HyperWorks Desktop Index Terms route. The exact page label is retained from Altair Help. Each link opens the complete source page; this summary omits long examples, error text and version history. [Back to the route catalog](./HYPERMESH_HWD_REFERENCE_CANDIDATES_2022_3.md).

| [*defineentitytypealiasname()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_defineentitytypealiasname.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `*defineentitytypealiasname (entity_type, alias_name)` | Defines an entity type alias name for a solver. | **Inputs:** entity_type The entity type to create the alias for. alias_name The alias name. |

Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. Details on the core data that can be queried and manipulated.This API allows external applications to link HyperMesh as a library and provides a limited set of functions and structures to access specific functionality. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. Details on the HMASCII format and associated commands.This section contains details on general HyperWorks Desktop scripting concepts, along with documentation on the Tcl commands that can be used to query and modify the database and GUI. Solver templates are ASCII data files containing HyperMesh Template Language Commands and HyperMesh Template Language Functions. The HyperMesh Card Image panel is used to define solver specific data and review card information. The format of the cards and the menus in the panel are defined in the export template, using HyperMesh Card Preview Commands. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. Learn the basics and discover the workspace.Discover HyperWorks Desktop functionality with interactive tutorials. Start HyperWorks Desktop and configure the applications. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. Details on the core data that can be queried and manipulated.This API allows external applications to link HyperMesh as a library and provides a limited set of functions and structures to access specific functionality. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. Solver templates are ASCII data files containing HyperMesh Template Language Commands and HyperMesh Template Language Functions. The HyperMesh Card Image panel is used to define solver specific data and review card information. The format of the cards and the menus in the panel are defined in the export template, using HyperMesh Card Preview Commands. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. Learn the basics and discover the workspace.Discover HyperWorks Desktop functionality with interactive tutorials. Start HyperWorks Desktop and configure the applications. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. Counts the entities in the database. | **Inputs:** entity type The type of entity to be counted. This parameter may be set to any of the entities in the database. config The configuration number of the entities to be counted. This parameter is used only if entity type is set to elements or loads. If set to zero, the entities are counted regardless of their configuration. type The type number of the entities to be counted. This parameter is used only if entity type is set to elements. If set to zero, all of the entities are counted regardless of their type. |

| [@elemcountperinclude()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_elemcountperinclude.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@elemcountperinclude (entity_type, config, type, include_id,component_id)` | This function returns the number of elements of a specific configuration and type for a specific component being exported to an include file. | **Inputs:** entity type The type of entity to be counted. Currently, this is restricted to be only elements. config The configuration of the elements to be counted. If set to zero, entities are counted regardless of their configuration. type The type of the elements to be counted. If set to zero, entities of the specified config are counted regardless of their type. include_id The ID of the include file. component_id The ID of the component. |

Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. Start HyperWorks Desktop and configure the applications. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. This option will export the CORD2R and CORD4R system in long format even if the template loaded is of regular format. A return of 1 means export system in long format, and 0 means export system in regular format. | Not stated |

Learn the basics and discover the workspace.Discover HyperWorks Desktop functionality with interactive tutorials. Start HyperWorks Desktop and configure the applications. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. Details on the core data that can be queried and manipulated.This API allows external applications to link HyperMesh as a library and provides a limited set of functions and structures to access specific functionality. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. | [@getattributearrayvaluebyinternalid2()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getattributearrayvaluebyinternalid2.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getattributearrayvaluebyinternalid2 (entity_type, id, attribute, index)` | Returns the internal ID of a 1D array attribute attached to an entity. | **Inputs:** entity_type The type of entity to query. id The internal ID of the entity to query. attribute The name of the solver attribute array, starting with $. index The index into the array to query, starting with 1. |

This can only be used inside of a *tables() block. | **Inputs:** row_index The index of the row in the table, starting from 0. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. triple_index The index of the triple value. Valid values are 0-2. |
| [@getcelltype()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getcelltype.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getcelltype (row_index, column_index)` | This function returns the type of a cell in a table. | [@getcellvalue()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getcellvalue.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getcellvalue (row_index, column_index)` | This function returns the values of a bool, double, float, int, string, unsigned int, or entity ID cell in a table. Start HyperWorks Desktop and configure the applications. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. Details on the core data that can be queried and manipulated.This API allows external applications to link HyperMesh as a library and provides a limited set of functions and structures to access specific functionality. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. The HyperMesh Card Image panel is used to define solver specific data and review card information. The format of the cards and the menus in the panel are defined in the export template, using HyperMesh Card Preview Commands. Start HyperWorks Desktop and configure the applications. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. Details on the core data that can be queried and manipulated.This API allows external applications to link HyperMesh as a library and provides a limited set of functions and structures to access specific functionality. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. Details on the HMASCII format and associated commands.This section contains details on general HyperWorks Desktop scripting concepts, along with documentation on the Tcl commands that can be used to query and modify the database and GUI. Solver templates are ASCII data files containing HyperMesh Template Language Commands and HyperMesh Template Language Functions. The HyperMesh Card Image panel is used to define solver specific data and review card information. The format of the cards and the menus in the panel are defined in the export template, using HyperMesh Card Preview Commands. | [@getentityvaluebyinternalid()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getentityvaluebyinternalid.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getentityvaluebyinternalid (entity_type, entity_id, data_name)` | Returns the value of an entity data name. | **Inputs:** entity_type The type of entity to query. entity_id The ID of the entity to query. data_name The data name to query. |
| [@getentityvalueinternalid()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getentityvalueinternalid.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getentityvalueinternalid (entity_type, entity_id, data_name)` | Returns the internal ID of an entity. | **Inputs:** entity_type The type of entity to query. entity_id The ID of the entity to query. data_name The entity data name to query. |
| [@getidoffsetvalue()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getidoffsetvalue.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getidoffsetvalue (entity_type, pool_id, include_file_id)` | Returns the ID offset value for an input entity with ID pool number in a specified include file ID. | **Inputs:** entity_type The type of entity to query. pool_id The ID pool number of the entity to query, if any. include_file_id The include file ID for which the offset is required. |
| [@getincludefullname()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getincludefullname.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getincludefullname (include_id)` | Returns the include file full name and path for a given include ID. | **Inputs:** include_id The ID of the include file to query. |
| [@getincludeidbyfilename()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getincludeidbyfilename.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getincludeidbyfilename (filename)` | Returns the ID of the include file if it exists, -1 if it does not, and 0 if it is the main include file. | **Inputs:** filename The name of the include file. |
| [@getincludereferenceflag()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getincludereferenceflag.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getincludereferenceflag (include_id, exception)` | Returns 1 if include_id is referenced into the main file, 0 otherwise. | **Inputs:** include_id The ID of the include. exception 0 - The include file reference depends on its export status. 1 - The include file reference depends on its export status, except for Include_Transform which is always referenced regardless its export status. |
| [@getinternalid()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getinternalid.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getinternalid (poolnumber, solverid)` | This command needs to be used in conjunction with the command @getentityvalue(), which returns the solver ID. You must know the pool number corresponding to an entity type of attribute. | **Inputs:** poolnumber The pool number to which the entity belongs. solverId The ID of the solver that you use. |
| [@getnewid()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_getnewid.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@getnewid (entity_type, id, pool_id, entity_type_check, pool_id_check)` | This function returns the values of a triple cell in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. | **Inputs:** entity_type The type of the entity to query. entity_id The ID of the entity to query. pool_id The ID pool number to which the queried entity belongs, if any. entity_type_check The type of the entity to check for a conflict with. pool_id_check The ID pool number to which the conflict entity belongs, if any. |

| [@gettablecelltriplevalue()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecelltriplevalue.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecelltriplevalue (table_id, row_index, column_index,triple_index)` | This function returns the values of a triple cell in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table. row_index The index of the row in the table, starting from 0. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. triple_index The index of the triple value. Valid values are 0-2. |
| [@gettablecelltype()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecelltype.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecelltype (table_id, row_index, column_index)` | This function returns the type of a cell in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table. row_index The index of the row in the table, starting from 0. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. |
| [@gettablecelltypebyinternalid()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecelltypebyinternalid.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecelltypebyinternalid (table_id, row_index, column_index)` | Returns the type of a cell in a table. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table entity to query. row_index The row of the table to query, starting from 0. column_index The colum of the table to query, starting from 0 |
| [@gettablecellvalue()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecellvalue.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecellvalue (table_id, row_index, column_index)` | This function returns the values of a bool, double, float, int, string, unsigned int, or entity ID cell in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table. row_index The index of the row in the table, starting from 0. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. |
| [@gettablecellvaluebyinternalid()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecellvaluebyinternalid.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecellvaluebyinternalid (table_id, row_index, column_index)` | Returns the values of a bool, double, float, int, string, unsigned int, or entity ID cell in a table. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table entity to query. row_index The row of the table to query, starting from 0. column_index The colum of the table to query, starting from 0 |
| [@gettablecolumnentitytype()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecolumnentitytype.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecolumnentitytype (table_id, column_index)` | This function returns the entity type ID of a column in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. The entity type ID can be converted to the entity type name using @getdatabaseentitytypename. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. |
| [@gettablecolumnlabel()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecolumnlabel.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecolumnlabel (table_id, column_index)` | This function returns the label of a column in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. |
| [@gettablecolumnsize()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecolumnsize.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecolumnsize (table_id, column_index)` | This function returns the size (number of rows) of a column in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. |
| [@gettablecolumnsizebyinternalid()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecolumnsizebyinternalid.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecolumnsizebyinternalid (table_id, column_index)` | Returns the size (number of rows) of a column in a table. | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table entity to query. column_index The colum of the table to query, starting from 0. |
| [@gettablecolumntype()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecolumntype.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecolumntype (table_id, column_index)` | This function returns the type ID of a column in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. The following values are valid for return: 1 - integer 2 - unsigned integer/entity ID 3 - float 4 - double 5 - bool 6 - string 7 - triple | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. |
| [@gettablecolumntypestring()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettablecolumntypestring.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettablecolumntype (table_id, column_index)` | This function returns the type string of a column in a table. This can be used both inside and outside of a *tables() block. The following values are valid for return: int unsignedint float double bool string triple HyperMesh entity types (for example elements, plies, components, and so on) | **Inputs:** table_id The ID of the table. column_index The index of the column in the table, starting from 0. |
| [@gettotalmass()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_gettotalmass.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@gettotalmass (?type?)` | Returns different total mass values of the model. | **Inputs:** type The type of mass to query. If not specified, the total mass is returned. structural - The total structural mass of the model. nonstructural - The total non-structural mass of the model. lumped - The total lumped mass of the model. rigid - The total rigid mass of the model. transferred - The total transferred mass of the model. engineering - The total engineering mass of the model. distributed - The total distributed mass of the model. |
| [@icedependentcount()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_icedependentcount.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@icedependentcount (independent_node_id)` | Returns the number of ICE dependent nodes for an independent node. | **Inputs:** independent_node_id The ID of the ICE independent node. |
| [@icedependentdof()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_icedependentdof.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@icedependentdof (independent_node_id, index)` | Returns ICE dependent node DOFs for an independent node. | **Inputs:** independent_node_id The ID of the ICE independent node. index The index of the dependent node, starting from 0. |
| [@icedependentnode()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_icedependentnode.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@icedependentnode (independent_node_id, index)` | Returns ICE dependent nodes for an independent node. | **Inputs:** independent_node_id The ID of the ICE independent node. index The index of the dependent node, starting from 0. |
| [@ignoresetswithsamenameascomps()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_ignoresetswithsamenameascomps.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@ignoresetswithsamenameascomps()` | This function returns the value of the “Do not export ELSET with same name as component” export option in the Abaqus profile or returns 1 if IGNORE_WRITING_SETS is passed via the FE export command. To ignore writing the *ELSET definition for the sets which have the same name as the components, the value returned by this command needs to be checked along with the template function to get the lists of sets with same name as components. | Not stated |
| [@keywordrenumber()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_keywordrenumber.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@keywordrenumber ("keyword")` | This function returns 1 if RENUMBER is passed with the queried keyword via *feoutputwithdata. | **Inputs:** keyword The keyword attribute name. |
| [@loadexistinmergedloadloadsteptable()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_loadexistinmergedloadloadsteptable.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@loadexistinmergedloadloadsteptable (id)` | This function returns 1 if the load exists in the table containing merged loads for a loadstep created by *createmergedloadloadsteptable(). | **Inputs:** id The ID of the load to query. |
| [@loadsinloadcollector()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_loadsinloadcollector.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@loadsinloadcollector (config_num, type_num, engineeringtype_num)` | This function returns the number of loads in a collector for a specified configuration, type, and engineering type. | **Inputs:** config_num The configuration number of the load being counted. If set to 0, loads are counted regardless of their configuration. type_num The type number of the loads being counted. If set to 0, all the loads are counted regardless of their type. engineeringtype_num The engineering type number of the loads being counted. If set to, -1: all the loads are counted regardless of their engineering type. -2: all the engineering loads are counted regardless of their engineering type. 0: all the classical loads are counted. > 0: all the loads of specified engineering type are counted. |

Learn the basics and discover the workspace.Discover HyperWorks Desktop functionality with interactive tutorials. Start HyperWorks Desktop and configure the applications. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. Details on the core data that can be queried and manipulated.This API allows external applications to link HyperMesh as a library and provides a limited set of functions and structures to access specific functionality. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. Details on the HMASCII format and associated commands.This section contains details on general HyperWorks Desktop scripting concepts, along with documentation on the Tcl commands that can be used to query and modify the database and GUI. Solver templates are ASCII data files containing HyperMesh Template Language Commands and HyperMesh Template Language Functions. The HyperMesh Card Image panel is used to define solver specific data and review card information. The format of the cards and the menus in the panel are defined in the export template, using HyperMesh Card Preview Commands. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. Solver templates are ASCII data files containing HyperMesh Template Language Commands and HyperMesh Template Language Functions. The HyperMesh Card Image panel is used to define solver specific data and review card information. The format of the cards and the menus in the panel are defined in the export template, using HyperMesh Card Preview Commands. Learn the basics and discover the workspace.Discover HyperWorks Desktop functionality with interactive tutorials. Start HyperWorks Desktop and configure the applications. Create, open, import, and save models. Control the view and display of your model in the modeling window. Use the Session Browser to view the current session and all the pages and windows it contains in a hierarchical display. Publish your HyperWorks Desktop session to HTML, PowerPoint, or Report files. Use the Parameters Browser to parameterize the contents of a session file or report template and automate a variety of analysis processes. Automate common workflows using the Process Manager. Explore, organize and manage your personal data, collaborate in teams, and connect to other data sources, such as corporate PLM systems to access CAD data or publish simulation data. Overview of external readers and the Altair Binary Format, the generic ASCII reader, running HyperWorks in batch mode, using Tcl/Tk commands, translators, and result math. Extensions are programs integrated with HyperWorks to enhance or extend the user experience. Altair Binary Format (ABF) is the standard binary format read by the program. A library called abflib allows you to create files in ABF. A generic ASCII reader is now available which allows you to bring custom results directly into HyperView. The Altair ASCII format is useful for reading the results of any in-house or propriety codes which are saved in a generic format. Batch mode allows you perform a series of operations outside the program interface. External resources can be registered in preference files, session files, or Templex files. If an external resource is registered in a program session, it is automatically registered in the session file when the session is saved. The HyperWorks Desktop scripting interface is a set of Tcl/Tk commands that follow a simple and consistent syntax. Translators are utilities that are run from the command line of the operating system to convert various types of files into different formats. XML commands in the Result Math Module.Record playback workflows in HyperView. These provide an overview of all additions, updates, and changes to the HyperMesh APIs for each release. Details on the core data that can be queried and manipulated.This API allows external applications to link HyperMesh as a library and provides a limited set of functions and structures to access specific functionality. FE input readers are C programs that read information from ASCII or binary finite element models files into the HyperMesh database. Details on the HMASCII format and associated commands.This section contains details on general HyperWorks Desktop scripting concepts, along with documentation on the Tcl commands that can be used to query and modify the database and GUI. Solver templates are ASCII data files containing HyperMesh Template Language Commands and HyperMesh Template Language Functions. The HyperMesh Card Image panel is used to define solver specific data and review card information. The format of the cards and the menus in the panel are defined in the export template, using HyperMesh Card Preview Commands. | [@repeatkeywordtitles()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_repeatkeywordtitles.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@autocreateproperty ()` | Returns 1 if the "repeat keyword titles" export option is enabled via *feoutputwithdata, 0 otherwise. | Not stated |
| [@sin()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_sin.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@sin (x)` | Trigonometric sine of x, where x is expressed in radians. | **Inputs:** x Value of type real. |
| [@sqrt()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_sqrt.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@sqrt (x)` | Returns the square root of a number. | Not stated |
| [@stringequal()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_stringequal.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@stringequal (string1, string2)` | Compares two strings, and returns 1 if they are equal; otherwise, 0. | **Inputs:** string 1 string 2 |
| [@stringinstring()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_stringinstring.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@stringinstring (string1, string2, case sensitive flag)` | Finds one string within another string. Returns 1 if the string is found, otherwise, 0. | **Inputs:** string1 The string that is searched. string2 The string to be found. case sensitive flag If set to 1, the search only succeeds if the case type of the two strings match exactly. For example, "Monday" will not match "monday". |
| [@stringlookup()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_stringlookup.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@stringlookup (key)` | Retrieves a value stored in a string lookup table. | **Inputs:** key Used to compare the keys found in the string lookup table. key can be a data name or a literal string enclosed in double quotes. |
| [@stringsplit()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_stringsplit.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@stringsplit (string,continuation_character)` | Splits the given string using the given continuation character. This is used in conjunction with the *field, *fieldleft or *fieldright commands. | **Inputs:** string The string to split. continuation_character The character to use on the continuation line. If set to blank, no continuation character will be used. |
| [@stringstartswithnumericcharacter()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_stringstartswithnumericcharacter.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@stringstartswithnumericcharacter (string)` | Checks if a string starts with a numeric character. | **Inputs:** string The string to check. |
| [@tan()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_tan.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@tan (x)` | Trigonometric tangent of x, where x is expressed in radians. | **Inputs:** (x) A value of type real. |
| [@vectorlookup()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_vectorlookup.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@vectorlookup (key, x comp, y comp, z comp)` | Retrieves a value stored in a vector lookup table. | **Inputs:** key Used to compare the keys found on the lookup table entries. x comp, y comp, z comp The components of the vector that are used to compare the vectors found on the lookup table entries. |

Solver templates are ASCII data files containing HyperMesh Template Language Commands and HyperMesh Template Language Functions. Retrieves a component of the vector stored in a lookup table. Retrieves a component of the vector stored in a lookup table. | **Inputs:** comp The component of the vector (1 - x, 2 - y, 3 - z) key Used to compare the keys found on the lookup table entries. |
| [@vectorlookupnotkey()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_vectorlookupnotkey.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@vectorlookupnotkey (key,x comp,y comp,z comp)` | Retrieves a value stored in a vector lookup table. | **Inputs:** key Used to compare the keys found in the lookup table entries. x comp,y comp,z comp The components of the vector that is used to compare the vectors found in the lookup table entries. |
| [@writehmcomments()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_writehmcomments.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@writehmcomments` | Returns 0 if HMCOMMENTS_SKIP is passed via *feoutputwithdata. | Not stated |
| [@writehmlegacycomments()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_writehmlegacycomments.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@writehmlegacycomments` | Returns 1 if EXPORT_LEGACY_COMP_COMMENTS is passed via *feoutputwithdata. | Not stated |
| [@writenastranoldcontact()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_writenastranoldcontact.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@writenastranoldcontact ()` | Returns the contact export option value for Nastran (MSC). Before 2017.1 for Nastran (MSC), both the "new" (BCBODY1, BCONECT BCTABL1) and "old" (BCBODY, BCTABLE) contacts were mapped to entities in HyperMesh. As a result, users were allowed to create decks with both the version of the solver cards and exported. That is not valid for the solver. Solver either expects a "new" or "old" deck. With 2017.1, users have the option to create "new" version of the solver cards and yet choose to export them as "old" contact cards. A return of 1 means old, and 0 means new. | Not stated |
| [@writesolverfieldnames()](https://2022.help.altair.com/2022.3/hwdesktop/hwd/topics/reference/hm/_writesolverfieldnames.htm) | HyperMesh Template Function | `@writesolverfieldnames ()` | Returns 0 if HMSOLVERFILEDNAMES_SKIP is passed via *feoutputwithdata. | Not stated |
